**No CSS at all**, which is worth stating because a count is exactly the kind of thing that acquires a rule: `.chip` has been declared in `app.css` since Slice 30 and the number's hook is `data-like-count`, a **data attribute rather than a class**, deliberately — `.record-` is §11.12's shared vocabulary prefix, and a hook borrowing it without declaring a rule would be a name in the shared namespace the stylesheet has never heard of (F-67's neighbourhood). **No schema change and no migration** — `0008` is unchanged and this slice writes nothing at all; it is a read and a chip. **No new read and no new write:** `ListLikeCountsAsync` shipped in Slice 57 and this slice is the caller it was named as waiting for. **No `REQUIREMENTS.md` edit**, on the v1.2 reasoning; no ADR amended, no `compose.yaml` edit, no `.slnx` edit, no `.csproj` edit, no new package, no `export.sh` edit, no `wwwroot/js/` edit, and **no new §16.3 scenario** — extending one that already presses a heart is cheaper than arranging a second guest, and it makes a stronger claim than either half alone. Predicted test count 1278 → **1279**: one assertion in `MenuItemReactionSurfaceContractTests`.

**v1.43 — 2026-08-24.** The half of the like a guest can see, and a number that was written three times in the one place a ruling had cleared. **§7** gains §11.1's control: it is in the item's **detail panel and never on its card**, and the reason is mechanical rather than aesthetic — a card *is* a `<button>`, the HTML parser closes the outer one when it meets a nested one, so the card splits in two and the half carrying the dish's name stops staging anything while nothing throws and the Razor compiles. **A toggle's accessible name does not move with its state** (constant word, `aria-pressed`, a glyph that changes *shape* rather than only colour), where the card one loop up legitimately does the opposite because it is a one-of-many **choice** rather than a toggle. **The press applies the write's answer rather than the tap's intent**, so `AlreadyInThatState` settles the control on the truth; and there is **no in-flight guard**, because a double-tap is the ordinary gesture on a heart and is the one `menu_item_reaction_current`'s identifier tie-break exists for (F-95). **§11.1** gains the control and the sentence that no like count is rendered to a guest anywhere. **This discharges the stronger of the two obligations Stage 5a re-opened** — a write with no caller — and one of the two reads; §11.4's count is Stage 5b's second half and is deliberately not first, because a count surface shipped ahead of the control would render a column no surface in the application could populate. **§16.3 gains scenario 21**, in the slice that built the control rather than a slice later: the reload is the whole scenario, since every step before it is satisfied by a `bool` on a component that no database hears about. That is **F-109**'s ruling on its first opportunity, against a control with **F-106**'s exact profile. **§16.4** gains one paragraph for the new `MenuItemReactionSurfaceContractTests` (**four assertions**, two of them rulings rather than mechanisms) and its counted-class census moves to **thirty-three**; the CI line moves twenty → twenty-one scenarios. **Appendix A** gains the Stage 5b row and **F-112**. **`app.css`** gains `.order-menu-detail-actions` and the `.order-menu-like*` set — `--touch-target` floor, no new breakpoint, and the pressed state carried by shape as well as by colour so the two states are distinguishable without colour vision.

**F-112, and it is a third generation rather than a repeat.** F-73 ruled that a census count in prose should be kept and moved by habit. F-89 reversed that after the habit failed twice, deleting both prose copies and leaving `MinimumCountedClasses` as the only place the number is written. The doc comment *on that constant* then grew a third copy — a narrative ending *"and now twenty-seven"*, one line above a constant reading **29**, guarding a §16.4 that stated **32**. Three numbers, two wrong, in the one place a ruling had gone to the trouble of clearing, and unreachable by the gate for the reason F-89 already recorded: it compares assertion counts per class against files, and a census is a count of paragraphs. The narrative is **deleted**, which is F-89's own remedy applied to the copy F-89 created, and the floor moves **29 → 33** with the census. **No gate**, on F-89's stated reasoning: the instrument that would catch a stale floor is an equality, and an equality would report a finding on every §16.4 paragraph that describes a class without enumerating one — which §16.4 legitimately does for the three response-header classes.

**Two changes, and v1.32's distinguishable-failure ruling governs both.** The control fails as a Razor compile error, as four named contract facts, or as one named scenario step; F-112 fails as `TestingSectionContractTests` naming a census below its floor. They fail in different projects, under different names, at different points in the pipeline. **`TableJourneys.SeatGuestAsync` moves into the harness** as a third change, and it is the loudest of the three by design: if the move is wrong, every scenario that seats a guest fails at once. It moved because a second scenario file needed it and a private method cannot be called from one — F-100's argument, applied to a test helper — and the old file keeps a one-line forwarder supplying its own patience constant, so its eight call sites are untouched. **No schema change and no migration** — `0008` is unchanged and this slice writes a type it already declares. **No new read and no new write:** both services shipped in Slice 57 and this slice is the caller they were named as waiting for. **No `REQUIREMENTS.md` edit**, on the v1.2 reasoning (rev 6 already asks for this menu); no ADR amended, no `compose.yaml` edit, no `.slnx` edit, no `.csproj` edit, no new package, no `export.sh` edit, and no `wwwroot/js/` edit. Predicted test count 1273 → **1278**: four assertions in the new `MenuItemReactionSurfaceContractTests`, one in the new `MenuReactionScenarios`.

**v1.42 — 2026-08-24.** The first stage of the menu enhancement that is not about a dish's own columns, and a paragraph that had been quietly asking for tests nobody should write. **Two changes, and v1.32's distinguishable-failure ruling governs both:** one is a migration with nine integration facts against a real database and one registration fact, the other is one contract fact over two text files. They fail in different projects with different names.

**The stage (5a).** **§7** records the like: `0008` adds `menu_item_reaction_event` and `menu_item_reaction_current` and touches nothing existing, which is `0003`'s cut for the third time. Four rulings are written down rather than left in the code — it is an **event table and not a row per like**, because the small schema makes unliking a `DELETE` and R§6.8 exists so that a record cannot be removed; there is **no `actor_person_identifier`**, because a reaction's subject is the person reacting and the column would be constrained to equal its neighbour forever; there is **no count column and no count view** (F-101's reasoning, in the one table that grows a row every time a thumb moves); and the fold's **identifier tie-break is load-bearing**, because two presses in one millisecond is an ordinary gesture rather than a contrivance and one transaction stamps one instant (§8.1) — it is an answer only because F-95 made the identifier factory ascend. **Both of Stage 5's open rulings are made:** the count is **staff-facing**, and a like requires **only a person**. And **a reaction publishes nothing**, which makes it the first menu write not behind `IMenuWorkflow` — §9's `MenuChanged` means *re-read the menu* and a like moves nothing a menu renders, so the standing wiring fact is narrowed to *every write that changes the menu*. **§8.2** and **§8.3** gain the table, its index and the fold. **§16.4** gains `MenuItemReactionTests` (nine assertions) and its counted-class census moves to **thirty-two**; `MenuWiringTests` moves 28 → 29 and `MenuEventVocabularyContractTests` 3 → 4. **Neither reaction service has a caller**, and Stage 5a says so: Stage 5b builds §11.4's count and §11.1's control, and the obligation is re-opened here exactly as Stage 4a re-opened it for the picture services.

**The finding (F-111).** The note beneath `menu_item_event`'s DDL described a table with five event types and two payload columns, twelve lines below a DDL block correctly showing eight and five — and required *\"integration tests must assert all ten combinations\"*, an obligation whose arithmetic was wrong even for the schema it was written against and which **§16.4 separately rules against writing** (F-47). The counts are deleted rather than corrected, the obligation is reversed to agree with §16.4, and the row names something executable: a fourth fact comparing every **named** `event_type` vocabulary this document quotes against the migrations, in both directions, subject computed on each side. No `REQUIREMENTS.md` edit — R§6.8 has required an append-only record since rev 1, so this is a mechanism catching up rather than new intent. No `OPERATIONS.md` edit, no ADR amended, no new package, no new §16.3 scenario (Stage 5a builds no surface for a browser to visit). Predicted test count 1260 → **1272**.

**v1.41 — 2026-08-23.** A red CI build, and the open item this feature had carried longer than any other. **Two changes, and v1.32's distinguishable-failure ruling governs both:** one is a `stackalloc` moved up seven lines, which fails as a CA2014 diagnostic from the compiler naming a file and a column in the end-to-end project; the other is what decides a stored media type, which fails as three named contract facts and one browser scenario. Nothing about the two can be confused for the other on a red run.

**The build break (F-108).** `PictureFixtures.Deflate` declared a four-byte `stackalloc` **inside** its stored-block loop. Stack memory is released when the method returns rather than when the iteration ends, so the frame grows once per pass. The interesting half is not the defect but the asymmetry that shipped it: `Directory.Build.props` deliberately leaves warnings non-fatal for a plain `dotnet build` — a fresh clone on tomorrow's SDK must build through analyzer drift rather than refusing at the door — and makes them errors under `-p:ContinuousIntegrationBuild=true`. So the local loop reported one line among eleven seconds of build output and CI reported a halt: `dotnet build` succeeded, `dotnet test` returned **1256** green exactly as predicted, and `scripts/ci_local.sh` stopped at step 5 with every step after it unrun. **No gate is added** (F-47, F-71): CA2014 is the gate, it ran and it blocked, and a lexical re-implementation of a Roslyn analyzer inside xUnit would be strictly weaker than the analyzer.

**The stage (4f, F-109 and F-110).** **§7** now records that **the bytes decide the media type and the browser's `Content-Type` is not read at all**. An `IFormFile.ContentType` is the operating system's extension map applied to a filename, so `application/octet-stream` for a genuine PNG is the ordinary answer from any system with no mapping — and every such upload was refused with a sentence blaming a format that was fine. Diagnosed in Slice 52, written down as a one-line fix, and carried for **six slices** on the objection that a surface identifying the format would make two write outcomes unreachable from the only form that produces them. That objection is answered rather than overridden: the surface passes the answer of *the same pure function the write consults*, so there is one decision procedure called twice, the write's two checks become true by construction, and both outcomes stay reachable at the write's own boundary where §16.2 reaches them. **Found on the way in (F-110):** the refusal spelled the vocabulary out in English and so did the paragraph above the form — a fourth and fifth declaration after §8.2's CHECK, the domain census and the derived `accept` hint, both invisible to every gate and both silently wrong the day a migration admits a fourth format. Both now render a derived list. **§16.4** gains `MenuItemImageContentTypeContractTests` (three assertions, all proven sensitive against the tree as it shipped with nothing planted) and its counted-class census moves to **thirty-one**; it is a class of its own rather than three more facts on the picture surface contract because that class is at twelve and `TestingSectionContractTests` says in its own summary that twelve is where a contract test has become two. **§16.3** gains scenario **20**, and the suite moves from nineteen to **twenty**. **Appendix A** gains **F-108**, **F-109** and **F-110**. No schema change, no migration, no new event type, no `REQUIREMENTS.md` edit, no `OPERATIONS.md` edit, no ADR amended, no new package. Predicted test count 1256 → **1260**.

**v1.40 — 2026-08-23.** The first finding an operator reported from the running product, and the number that finally had to leave the database. **Two changes, and v1.32's distinguishable-failure ruling governs both:** one is a misplaced tag with a new gate and two browser scenarios, the other is a browser-side downscaler; they fail with different test names in different projects.

**The defect (F-106).** `ManageMenuItem.razor` carried `<ValidationMessage>` one line *outside* its `EditForm`, inside `@if (_picture is not null)`. A sibling of an `EditForm` receives no cascading `EditContext` and `ValidationMessage` throws without one, so the attaching POST — which renders while `_picture` is still null — succeeded and committed, and the **redirected GET answered 500**. Every administrator view of a decorated item answered 500 thereafter, including the one carrying the Remove button, so nothing in the product could undo it. **§16.4** gains `EditContextConsumerContractTests` and the reasoning for why the rule is absolute rather than a matter of taste. **§16.3** gains scenarios **18** and **19** — the first that put a picture on a dish through a browser — and the paragraph explaining why the fixture image is *generated* rather than carried, which is the objection the plan had deferred this scenario under four times.

**The feature (F-107, Stage 4e).** §8.2's half-megabyte cap and a phone camera's four megabytes meant the honest answer to almost every real upload was *too large*. **§7** now records that the browser resizes an oversized picture before the multipart form posts it, that this is an **optimisation and never a check** — every refusal is still the write service's and the schema's — and that the budget is read out of `pg_constraint` at render time rather than restated, so `0006` remains the only place in this repository that says how large a picture may be. **§16.4** records the two new facts on the picture surface contract, the second of which computes the bound out of the migration and forbids it anywhere under `src/`. **§11.11 is unchanged and that is deliberate**: `createImageBitmap` takes the `File` directly and `canvas.toBlob` returns a `Blob` that becomes a `File`, so nothing produces a `blob:` or `data:` URL and the policy needed nothing (F-49's lesson applied forward rather than after the fact). **Appendix A** gains **F-106** and **F-107**. No schema change — Stage 4e adds no migration, which is the point of reading the cap rather than moving it. No `REQUIREMENTS.md` edit, on the v1.2 reasoning: R§1 has required the product to be usable from a phone since rev 1, and a menu picture nobody can upload from one is a mechanism catching up with a contract this tree already stated.

**v1.39 — 2026-08-19.** The archive that shipped v1.38 did not compile, and the history of a picture had never been readable. **Three changes, and v1.32's distinguishable-failure ruling governs all three**: F-104 fails as `RZ1010` from the Razor compiler naming a file and a column, Stage 4d fails as named integration and wiring assertions, and F-105 fails as one contract fact naming the event type it could not find a sentence for. Nothing about the three can be confused for one another on a red run.

**The defect that blocked everything (F-104).** `TableOrderSurface.razor` acquired `@{ int headingIndex = -1; }` **inside** the `else` block that renders the grouped menu, where Razor is already parsing C# — so the transition *from markup into code* had no markup to leave, and every project depending on `MyRestaurant.WebApplication` never ran. That is **F-82's mechanism for the fourth time**, and its expense here is exact and nameable: the two facts that would have reported F-105 are in the project that did not build. **What earns it a ledger row rather than a correction is that the correct and the incorrect form are indistinguishable on the line itself.** The three other `@{` blocks in this tree are correct and each sits directly inside an **element**; this one sat inside a **code block**, and the nearest enclosing construct is what decides which of the two an `@` is. **No gate is added** (F-47, F-71): markup-versus-code context is not decidable from text without a Razor parser, the Razor parser decides it in three seconds, and a second implementation inside the suite would be two parsers for one rule with the worse one blocking. The reason is written beside the statement instead, because the next person to reach for `@{` there will be reading that file rather than this changelog. **§18 gains the residual**: brace balance and tag-tree walks have no notion of transition context, and nothing in this repository other than the compiler does.

**The stage (4d).** **§7** and **§11.4** gain the picture history. `IMenuItemImageEventLog` and `DapperMenuItemImageEventLog` read `menu_item_image_event`, which `0006` created and three slices wrote to — four event types deep as of `0007` — while nothing in the tree could read it: §16.4 had recorded that absence by name on each of those slices, on the rule that a read arrives with the surface that renders it, exactly as `IMenuSectionEventLog` arrived with the section editor. **The reader has one deliberate divergence from its two siblings and it is the whole design of the read: it must not join `menu_item_image`.** Every other reader in this family joins the row its events are about; here that row is *frequently gone*, because a replace mints a new identifier and deletes the old row so that §7's `Cache-Control: immutable` is a true statement and a removal deletes it outright — so an INNER JOIN returns a history that silently **begins at the current photograph**, and a LEFT JOIN adds a column null on every row but the newest. `0006` declared no foreign key on that column precisely so this can be true, and it is asserted rather than trusted to the comment that says so. **§11.4's panel renders whether or not a picture is attached now**, and that is normative rather than incidental: *no picture now, three of them previously* is the state this surface could not describe, and hiding the panel when nothing is attached would hide it in the one case somebody opens the page to ask about. It renders **no identifier and no link** — a URL for a replaced picture answers 404 by design, so a link would be a link that mostly does not work, and a bare UUIDv7 in a cell is a fact about this schema rather than about the restaurant — and what a picture **was** goes inside the sentence for the two types that carry it rather than into two columns empty on half the rows, which would put a `data-label` reading *Format* beside nothing on the row somebody came to read. **§16.4** gains a paragraph for the new `MenuItemImageEventLogTests` (6 assertions) and `MenuWiringTests` moves **27 → 28**; the counted-class census moves to **twenty-nine**.

**The defect this document was carrying about itself (F-105).** §7 said `menu_item_image_event` mirrors every change to it — *`attached`, `replaced`, `removed`*, CHECK-bound by **two** named biconditionals. `0007` had made it four types and three, one slice earlier. **The aggravating circumstance is three sections above it**: §7's paragraph on `menu_item_event` states in bold that its vocabulary is *not counted in prose anywhere* and that the list there is the only copy — a rule written down after F-77 and then not applied to the table this project added next. F-77's shape for the seventh time, and the second time in three slices that the stale artefact was a **specification or design paragraph** rather than a comment (F-101, F-103). Found by reading §7 in order to write Stage 4d's reader, which is **F-93's** timing for the fifth time. The list is **corrected** on F-77's cheaper direction, the **count is deleted** in favour of *one named paired biconditional per payload column* — a rule no migration can falsify — and **the row names something executable**, because a corrected sentence is worth nothing when the next migration will be written by somebody who has not read it: `MenuEventVocabularyContractTests` moves **2 → 3** with a fact that every type the migration admits has a sentence on the one surface that renders that log. **Its subject is the surface rather than the write service**, since that service's four type constants are `private` and widening an API for a test's convenience is the wrong trade — and because §11.4 falls back to the raw string for an unrecognised type **by design**, so a missing arm throws nothing, logs nothing and appears only as a cell reading `alt_text_changed` where a sentence belongs. **That is F-80's symptom exactly.** The other direction is deliberately unasserted (F-41).

**What is deliberately not here.** **No browser downscaling**, for a fifth slice, and it is now the only thing standing between this feature and the person who asked for it: a phone camera produces four megabytes against §8.2's 512 KiB cap, so the answer to most uploads is still *too large* and the pictures those uploads would have become are the feature a guest sees. It is named rather than quietly carried, and it is named as **the next slice** rather than as a deferral. **No §16.3 scenario touches a picture**: the seventeen are unchanged, and a picture scenario needs a fixture image the harness has no way to produce. **No migration, no schema change, no new event type, no CSS** — `.record-list`, `.manage-subheading` and `data-label` are all §11.12's existing vocabulary and `.manage-subheading` was already declared and already used by `ManageSitting.razor`. **No new control of any kind on §11.4's item page**, so F-93 asks nothing of this slice: a read-only table acquires no barrier selector. **No `REQUIREMENTS.md` edit**, on the v1.2 reasoning; no ADR amended, no `compose.yaml` edit, no `.slnx` edit, no `.csproj` edit, no new package, no `export.sh` edit. Predicted test count 1242 → **1250**: six assertions in the new `MenuItemImageEventLogTests`, one in `MenuWiringTests`, one in `MenuEventVocabularyContractTests`.
**v1.38 — 2026-08-19.** The picture a guest can finally see, and the sentence a screen reader reads instead of it. Two changes, and v1.32's distinguishable-failure ruling governs: Stage 4c fails as a missing thumbnail in a browser or a named assertion in a contract test, and **F-103** fails as one tree-wide gate over `<img>` tags that either finds a missing `alt` or does not.

**The stage (4c).** **§7** gains three paragraphs, replacing the one that said §11.1 rendered no picture. The card is **re-laid out rather than decorated** — a 4rem square thumbnail beside the name, cropped under `object-fit: cover` because nothing in this stack can resize an image and a portrait photograph at `height: auto` would put back the card height the crop exists to bound; the whole frame is in the detail panel, which §11.1 has named the surface images are read on since Slice 39. `loading` is per **heading** rather than per card, because how many cards sit above the fold depends on description length, viewport width and the reader's text size, none of which the server knows. **`IMenuItemImageDirectory.ListAsync` has the caller it was named for**, closing an obligation carried by name for three slices, and it is read beside the menu and re-read on the same `MenuChanged` so that a picture dictionary cannot become a second stale copy of the menu. **§8.2** gains `menu_item_image.alt_text` (`NOT NULL DEFAULT ''`, `''` means *none*) and `menu_item_image_event.new_alt_text`, with `0007` widening the vocabulary **by name** — the return `0006` collected on naming every constraint it declared, against `0004`'s DO block over `0001`'s unnamed CHECKs. **It widened neither existing biconditional, which is the surprising half**: a caption is not a fact about the file, so `alt_text_changed` sits outside both right-hand sides and passes each with NULL. Three rulings are decisions rather than implementation: the column is **on the picture and not on `menu_item`**, because alternative text describes a photograph and a column on the item would outlive the picture it described; **a replace carries it forward** and writes no event for the carry, since resetting to `''` would strip alternative text off a guest's menu as a side effect of improving a photograph; and it is **its own verb** rather than a parameter on the attach, because the upload form requires a file and a caption settable only there would make a typo cost a new identifier and a year of invalidated caches. **§11.1** and **§11.4** gain the two surfaces, the second with a caption editor carrying **no file input** and rendering the caption as visible text beside `alt=""`. **§16.4** gains a second paragraph on the picture surface class and its count moves **six → ten**; `MenuItemImageTests` moves **11 → 15** and `MenuWiringTests` **26 → 27**; the counted-class census stays at **twenty-eight**, because the guest surface joined the existing class rather than founding a new one — the route-helper claim is one claim over two files. **`app.css`** gains `.order-menu-body`, the thumbnail and the panel's picture, and `max-width: 100%` on the last of those is load-bearing rather than presentational for `.manage-picture-image`'s reason.

**F-103.** The plan's own justification for the column was **false as written**, and it was found on the way into the slice that would have shipped it — F-93's timing for the fourth time. It said an `<img>` with no alternative text on a menu is a card a screen reader renders as nothing, which conflates a **missing** `alt` attribute (a screen reader announces the URL, here a bare UUID) with `alt=""` (it skips an image whose surroundings already say what it is). On §11.1's card the surroundings do, because the card is a button holding the dish's name and price as text, so `''` is the **correct** value for most pictures and the column earns its place only for the ones that say something a name does not. The column is kept for that narrower reason and the sentence is corrected rather than deleted, because the cheaper direction was available (F-77's habit). The consequence for the markup is that the `<img>` is **last in the document and first on the screen** — a button's accessible name is computed in document order, so a captioned picture placed first would announce its own description before the dish it describes. **The row names something executable**: one gate asserting every `<img>` in the tree carries the attribute, which is the shape this class of defect needs, because the right value is usually empty and therefore the wrong markup is invisible on every screen.

**What is deliberately not here.** **No §16.3 scenario touches a picture**: the seventeen are unchanged, and a picture scenario needs a fixture image the harness has no way to produce — inventing bytes inside the harness would be a test arranging what it asserts about. It is now the largest end-to-end gap in the menu after the two resequencing verbs, and it is named rather than quietly skipped. **No `menu_item_image_event` reader**, so §11.4 still cannot show a picture's history and `alt_text_changed` is written and read by nothing but its tests — the same read-with-no-caller rule, carried. **No `SchemaMigrationRunnerTests` edit**: `0007` adds columns rather than relations, and its biconditional is exercised behaviourally. **No `REQUIREMENTS.md` edit, no `OPERATIONS.md` edit, no ADR amended, no `compose.yaml` edit, no `.slnx` edit, no `.csproj` edit** — `Migrations/*.sql` is a glob — **no new package, no `export.sh` edit, and no browser downscaling**, which is the open question of this stage for a fourth slice and now the thing that decides whether the feature is usable by the person who asked for it. Predicted test count 1233 → **1242**: four assertions in `MenuItemImageTests`, four in `MenuItemImageSurfaceContractTests`, one in `MenuWiringTests`.

**v1.37 — 2026-08-18.** The picture a menu could finally show, and a count of seven that said six. Two changes, and the ruling that lets them ride together is v1.32's: they fail in **distinguishable** ways. F-102 is the deletion of a number from a comment and cannot fail at all. The stage fails as a compiler error naming a Razor handler, a named assertion in a new contract test, or a 404 in a browser.

**The stage (4b).** **§7** gains four paragraphs: the route, the transport, the assertion about §11.11, and what was deliberately left for 4c. `GET /menu/image/{menu_item_image_identifier}` is an **anonymous** minimal-API endpoint — anonymous because §11.1's guest menu is what it exists for and a guest at a table may not have signed in yet (§4.3) — answering 404 for a picture since replaced or removed and setting `Cache-Control: public, max-age=31536000, immutable`, which is **true rather than hopeful** because the route is keyed on the image and a replace mints a new identifier. It carries **no §3.5 obligations exemption**, and the distinction from the clock and the source offer is stated: those are asked for *by* a page a locked-down principal is looking at, this is a subresource of a page such a principal never reached. `AttachMenuItemImageAsync` and `RemoveMenuItemImageAsync` come behind **`IMenuWorkflow`**, which **discharges the obligation v1.36 deliberately re-opened**, and the publish is keyed on the **two outcomes that wrote a row** rather than on "not a refusal", because that enum's refusal set has grown twice already and a new member must not become an announcement by default.

**The transport question is settled, and neither of the two answers the plan named is the one taken.** A plain `enctype="multipart/form-data"` form posts to **the page itself** under an ordinary `@formname`, and the handler reads the part out of `HttpContext.Request.Form.Files` — which costs nothing, because Blazor's static form handling has already read the body in order to find `_handler` and dispatch there. The page stays static SSR, antiforgery stays the framework's, post/redirect/get is unchanged, and no second endpoint acquires an authorization rule that has to agree with a page's; what is given up is model binding for exactly one field, and that is the field the model binder refuses. **No number is added in C#** — §8.2's named CHECK is still the only cap, reported by constraint name, and the ceiling on what this process buffers is the one every other POST in this application already has, so the picture form declares no transport limit of its own. **The browser's declared media type is handed on unaltered** and the surface deliberately does not identify the format itself, even though `ImageFormat` is public: the write is the one place that decides what an image is, and a surface that pre-judged would leave two of that verb's outcomes unreachable from the only form that can produce them.

**§11.11 needed no edit and that is now asserted rather than assumed**, which is the promise v1.36 recorded and carried. `ContentSecurityPolicyContractTests` gains one fact in two halves: that `img-src` admits `'self'`, and that every `<img>` in the tree is still same-origin — the second being the one that would fail, since a CDN or a thumbnail service is exactly what somebody reaches for when a menu grows pictures. **§16.4** gains one paragraph for the new `MenuItemImageSurfaceContractTests` and its counted-class census moves to **twenty-eight**; the six assertions there all guard the same silent failure, in which the form renders, the button submits, the page redirects and the file is simply not there. **Appendix A** gains the Stage 4b row and **F-102**. **`app.css`** gains three `.manage-picture*` rules, and one declaration in them is load-bearing rather than presentational: nothing in this stack can resize an image, so an `<img>` with no `max-width` renders at whatever a camera produced and would make the document wider than a 375px viewport — §16.3 scenario 16 would fail on a page whose every control is correctly placed.

**The defect (F-102).** `AttachMenuItemImageOutcome`'s own summary said *six answers* and the enum has **seven** members, three lines below the sentence counting them. It is **deleted rather than corrected**, on **F-77's** standing ruling, and it is that ruling's sixth shape — after a version header, a variable four documents agreed about, a port three helpers dialled, a touch target written eight pixels short, a class census in three places and an index counting one thing while saying another. It was found by reading the type in order to write the surface that renders one sentence per member, which is **F-93's** timing for the third time. **No gate is added**, on F-41 and F-47: a rule forbidding a number near an enum would report findings on every correct comment that mentions one. The residual shrinks by accident rather than by design — the handler now switches over the outcomes, so a member with no sentence is a missing `case` the compiler can be asked about rather than a missing paragraph nothing reads.

**What is deliberately not here.** **§11.1 renders no picture**, and that is Stage 4c rather than an omission: an image per card doubles the height of a menu on a 375px screen, so the card is re-laid out rather than decorated, and a guest's `<img>` wants an `alt_text` column — one `ALTER` with a `DEFAULT ''` on `0004`'s precedent — because a picture there may carry something its own name does not. §11.4's panel needs neither and renders `alt=""` deliberately, the picture sitting under the item's own name in the page's `<h1>`. So **`IMenuItemImageDirectory.ListAsync` is a read with no caller**, named on the same rule that named the write and weaker than it, because an unread read cannot change anything without telling anybody. **No §16.3 scenario**: the seventeen are unchanged, and a picture scenario needs a fixture file the harness has no way to produce yet — named rather than quietly skipped. **No schema change, no migration, no new event type, no `REQUIREMENTS.md` edit, no `OPERATIONS.md` edit, no ADR amended, no `compose.yaml` edit, no `.slnx` edit, no new package and no `export.sh` edit.** Predicted test count 1223 → **1233**: six assertions in the new `MenuItemImageSurfaceContractTests`, three in `MenuWiringTests`, one in `ContentSecurityPolicyContractTests`.

**v1.36 — 2026-08-18.** The picture a menu could not carry, and three columns a design document had already got wrong. Two changes, and the ruling that lets them ride together is the one v1.32 stated: they fail in **distinguishable** ways. F-101 is the removal of three columns from a schema that had not been written yet, so it cannot fail at all — there is nothing to break in a table that never existed. The stage itself fails as a DbUp error naming `0006`, a compiler error naming a positional record, or a named assertion in one of two new test classes. Neither symptom can be mistaken for the other.

**The stage (4a).** **§7** gains five paragraphs and **§8.2** gains two tables: `menu_item_image` and `menu_item_image_event`, created by `0006_menu_item_images.sql`, read and written by `IMenuItemImageDirectory` and `IMenuItemImageAdministration`. **ADR-0015** is new and carries the rulings. The bytes are `bytea` rather than a volume, on **F-38's** argument — §15 *defines* a recovery set as two artefacts and `restore_drill.sh` rehearses both, so a third means editing that definition, `backup.sh`, `restore.sh`, the drill and the runbook, after which an operator who takes one more backup the old way holds a set that restores an application whose menu has no pictures in it. Object storage is refused one register out, on R§1's self-hosted premise. **The direction of reversibility is what decided it rather than the cost**: `bytea` → volume reads rows and writes files, volume → `bytea` cannot find the files. `0006` is `0003`'s cut a second time — two tables, nothing existing touched, green by construction rather than by inspection, and `OrderTestWorld` needed no edit because `TRUNCATE … CASCADE` on `menu_item` reaches both. Four schema rulings are recorded rather than left to be inferred: one image per item as **`UNIQUE` on the referencing column** so a gallery is a later migration rather than a redesign; **a replace mints a new identifier and deletes the old row**, because §7's route is keyed on the image so that `Cache-Control: immutable` is a *true* statement rather than a hope; **a removal deletes the row**, a stated exception to §6.8 whose argument is that the history is in the log and the bytes are not history; and **the log references `menu_item`, not the image**, with the image named as a bare `uuid` — the opposite of `0005`'s ruling about `new_menu_section_identifier`, and opposite because the row an image event describes is gone by design. `ImageFormat` in **`Domain`** checks the declared media type against the bytes' own signature — PNG's eight bytes, JPEG's marker, and **both halves** of WebP's RIFF header, since `RIFF` alone is an AVI or a WAV — and it is in `Domain` on **F-100's** argument, the cases worth asserting being the malformed ones. **The media-type vocabulary is declared twice and the agreement is asserted behaviourally**, which is F-80's repair made stronger: a real file of every recognised format is attached and the database must accept it, so the two agreeing on paper while nothing can be stored is also a failure. **§16.4** gains three paragraphs and its counted-class census moves to **twenty-seven**. **§8.1** names `0006`; **Appendix A** gains **F-101** and the Stage 4a row.

**The defect (F-101), and it is the mechanism rather than the columns.** The plan's Stage 4 DDL sketch specified `byte_length` — which is `octet_length(bytes)`, one fact written twice in a place a single `UPDATE` can make disagree, **F-65's mechanism for the seventh time** — plus `pixel_width` and `pixel_height`, which are worse than duplicates because **nothing in this stack can measure them**: the same stage's own text says the server stores what it is given, so both numbers could only ever have been the client's word recorded in the indicative, in a column a reader would take for something the database knew. All three are dropped **before `0006` was written**. What earns a row is where the sketch lived: three gates read that document for structure, tables and version and none reads a fenced SQL block for meaning, so a DDL sketch is unchecked prose carrying the authority of a schema — and the artefact it becomes is the one artefact this project never edits (F-34). **No gate is added over the plan's SQL blocks**, on F-41 and F-47: a document that sketches a schema in order to argue about it legitimately contains shapes the tree does not have, so a gate reading them for agreement would report findings on a correct document. The same reading is applied once more on the way in: **the 512 KiB cap is written only in the DDL**, reported by constraint *name* rather than by a second copy of the number, with the integration fact reading the bound back out of `pg_get_constraintdef`.

**What is deliberately not here, and one obligation deliberately re-opened.** **No surface**: the route that serves the bytes, the administrator's form, and §11.1's thumbnail are Stage 4b's, and one is a real open design question rather than a deferral — §11.4's pages are static SSR, so a file upload there is not the interactive `InputFile` shape. So two data-access services exist with no caller outside their tests, which **re-opens the obligation Slice 43 closed** and is named on every slice until 4b discharges it; it is the weaker form, because **nothing is added behind `IMenuWorkflow`** and no surface can change a picture without announcing it for the simple reason that no surface can change one. **No `§11.11` edit and no `ContentSecurityPolicyContractTests` edit**: `default-src 'self'` declares no `img-src`, so bytes served from this origin are already covered — and because a CSP is the one configuration that becomes wrong by editing a file it does not mention (F-49), "no change needed" is carried to Stage 4b to be **asserted** with the route rather than left true by accident. **No `OPERATIONS.md` edit**, which is ADR-0015 paying out on arrival: the runbook, both scripts and the drill are untouched because the recovery set is still two artefacts, where a volume would have meant editing five files whose subject is getting a restaurant's data back. **No `REQUIREMENTS.md` edit**, on the v1.2 reasoning: rev 6 already asks for this menu and R§6.8 has required an append-only mirror of every menu change since rev 1. No read changed, no existing event type, no ADR amended, no `compose.yaml` edit, no `.slnx` edit, no new package, no `export.sh` edit and **no CSS at all** — nothing renders a picture yet. Predicted test count 1202 → **1223**: eight assertions in the new `ImageFormatTests`, eleven in the new `MenuItemImageTests`, and two theory rows on `SchemaMigrationRunnerTests`' relation census, whose assertion count stays at 7 because a theory row is not a `[Fact]` (F-90).

**v1.35 — 2026-08-18.** The last surface that read the menu flat, and the rule that had nowhere to be tested. Two changes and they are **one change**, which is why this version does not need v1.32's distinguishable-failure argument: the grouping the “86” panel needed could not be reached from a second component until it left the first one, so extracting it and consuming it are the same edit. **§11.2** becomes normative about the panel: menu items are **grouped under their headings** in the order §7 stores, **headings guests cannot see are listed and marked**, an empty heading is absent, a heading's own description is deliberately not rendered, and there is **no per-heading toggle**. The hidden-heading rule is stated as *required rather than permitted*, and the argument is §7's own: deactivating a heading does not deactivate its items, and this panel is the only surface that can read or change an item's availability — so a hidden heading dropped here is a run of dishes the kitchen can neither 86 nor bring back until somebody switches the heading on. **§16.4** gains one paragraph and its counted-class census moves to **twenty-five**. **Appendix A** gains **F-100**, and it is a finding rather than an enhancement because of what it is a *second* instance of: the walk carrying §11.1's grouping and §7's asymmetry sat in a private property inside a Razor component for ten slices, where §16.1 guarantees nothing but §16.3 could see it, and `KitchenQueue`'s own summary had already written the rule down — *a rule that can only be checked by rendering a component is a rule nobody checks*. It became a defect rather than a preference the moment a second surface needed the same walk with the opposite rule, because the alternative to moving it was pasting it (F-59). The smaller half is the walk's own comment: it said the first row of each run and the code read the last, harmless only because the INNER JOIN makes the two readings agree, which is why nothing could falsify it — **made true rather than deleted**, F-77's habit taking the cheaper direction for once. **No schema change, no migration, no read changed, no new event type, no workflow verb, no `REQUIREMENTS.md` edit**, no ADR edit, no `compose.yaml` edit, no `.slnx` edit, no new package, and **no `app.css` edit** — the panel's two new rules are component-local, `.kitchen-` being deliberately absent from the shared-vocabulary prefix list, and neither reaches for a `.menu-group` name that belongs to §11.4's index. **ADR-0014's Context sentence calling the panel a flat `<ul>` is deliberately left as written**, on the precedent of the guest `<select>` named in the same clause and made obsolete in Slice 39: an ADR's Context records the world that motivated the decision. Predicted test count 1191 → **1202**: eleven assertions, all in the new `MenuGroupingTests`.

**v1.34 — 2026-08-18.** The sentence a guest could not read, and a number a test got wrong about a tree that was right. Two changes, and the ruling that lets them ride together is the one v1.32 stated and v1.33 applied: they fail in *distinguishable* ways. The arithmetic repair is one assertion in one file and cannot fail at all — it is the removal of a failure — and the read widening fails as a compiler error naming a positional constructor, an integration fact naming a column, or a scenario naming a paragraph the browser did not find. Neither symptom can be mistaken for the other.

**F-99 is the failing assertion and it is worth reading for the mechanism rather than the number.** Slice 48's cross-heading isolation fact rotated three items — which moves all three — and asserted the two events a *reversal* writes, having taken the list from one sibling fact and the count from another. It reported `Expected: 2, Actual: 3` against a verb behaving exactly as §7 specifies, so the one red assertion in an otherwise green run of 1190 was a defect **in the test's arithmetic**, in the direction that reports a defect that does not exist. The count becomes 3 and the arithmetic is written into the fact's summary rather than left to be re-derived, together with what the rotation costs: three moved of three listed cannot also witness the per-row no-op rule, which stays where a reversal can see it. **No new gate** (F-47, F-71) — the suite named the fact, the file, the line and both numbers on the first run after it landed, and a gate over the arithmetic inside an assertion is the assertion.

**The menu progress closes the last outstanding piece of Stage 3's guest menu.** §11.1 renders **a heading's own description beneath its name**, and a heading with none renders no paragraph rather than an empty one. `MenuItemSummary` gains `MenuSectionDescription` as one more aliased column on the INNER JOIN that has carried `MenuSectionName` since `0005`, which **reverses a sentence four places in this tree carried for nine slices** — that a surface needing the heading's description would read the section directory instead. The reversal is argued on correctness rather than cost: two reads happen at two instants, so a heading renamed between them renders its new name above its old sentence, and no lock a guest's picker could take would prevent that. One row of one query cannot disagree with itself. **The publish needed no edit**, which is Slice 40's ruling paying out — `MenuChanged` meant *re-read the menu* before there was anything new to read, and a workflow that had decided which columns were worth announcing would have had to be edited here on the day the surface changed. Deliberately **no `aria-describedby`** on the list: expressing "no description, no attribute" needs an attribute whose value is null, which this tree has no precedent for, so the honest alternative was rendering the list twice; the paragraph sits between the heading and the list in document order instead. **§16.3's scenario 17 gains the assertion and needed no new arrangement** — it has created *Starters* with a description and *Puddings* without one since Slice 40, so both halves of the rule are asserted from what was already there, and it is now the only place `menu_section.description` is carried from the form that typed it to the phone that reads it. **§7** and **§11.1** record the render; **§16.4**'s `MenuDirectoryTests` count moves 7 → 8 and its resequence paragraph records F-99's ruling; **Appendix A** gains **F-99** and the Stage 3 row. No schema change and no migration — the column has existed since `0005`, and this is a read learning to select it. No new read, no ADR edit, no `compose.yaml` edit, no `.slnx` edit, no `REQUIREMENTS.md` edit (rev 6 already asks for this menu), no `export.sh` edit, and the counted-class census does not move: no test class is added. One CSS rule is added and it declares nothing new — the same three declarations `.menu-group-description` already carries, because it is the same sentence read by a different person. Predicted test count 1190 → **1191**: one assertion in `MenuDirectoryTests`.

**v1.33 — 2026-08-18.** The rule that caught its own documentation, and the ordering verb one register down. Two changes, and the ruling that lets them ride together is the one v1.32 stated: they fail in *distinguishable* ways. A prose defect fails as a named line number out of `scripts/check_repository.sh` with no test involved; a verb defect fails as a named assertion in one of two files. Neither symptom can be mistaken for the other, so a red run answers its own first question.

**The gate (F-98), which was red on arrival of this slice rather than caused by it.** v1.31 added §16.4's paragraph on `ContextDumpExclusionContractTests`, and that paragraph explained why the archived build log needs the platform-state exemption by **reproducing the forbidden claim verbatim**. Gate 3 of `scripts/check_repository.sh` greps every tracked non-record file for exactly that phrasing, so this document asserted a repository setting it cannot check, in a sentence whose subject was that very rule, and the governance gate failed on a tree where nothing else was wrong. The claim is now **described rather than quoted**. Adding this document to `RECORD_FILES` was considered and **rejected**: it is the largest non-record file in the tree, and exempting 449 KiB of normative prose to accommodate one clause is F-46's argument — a rule stated as a rule and enforced as a list of exceptions is enforced as a list of exceptions. **No new gate**, on F-47 and F-71: the existing one caught this on the first run after the sentence landed, and a gate asserting that gate 3's subject does not describe itself is a monument. The mechanism is worth more than the fix and is recorded as such — *a paragraph documenting why a forbidden string is permitted somewhere is the paragraph most likely to contain that string*, so a gate over authored text is tripped by its own documentation before it is tripped by a defect.

**The verb (Stage 3b).** **§7** records `ResequenceMenuItemsAsync`: a **heading** and the whole ordering of the items under it, `0…n-1` assigned from the list, one `reordered` event per item that actually moved, a non-permutation **refused whole**. It is a slice of its own rather than a widening of v1.32's for the reason Slice 47 named it as: the shape is identical and the table is not — `menu_item_event` has five named paired CHECKs where `menu_section_event` has three, and a verb is a write to a table rather than an idea about ordering. **Three things the design did not settle are settled here.** The heading is a *parameter* rather than inferred from the list, because a position is a position *within* a section and sending the whole menu would renumber the puddings because somebody moved a drink. An **unknown heading returns `MenuItemSetChanged`** through the ordinary permutation comparison rather than through a fourth outcome — it has no items under it, and from the surface's side an unknown heading and a stale item set are one fact, *this page is stale, reload it*. And **the section row is deliberately not locked**, which is the one substantive difference from the section verb: a concurrent create or refile appends at `MAX + 1` computed from the very positions this verb is holding, so `n` rows with maximum `m` put the arrival at `m + 1 ≥ n`, strictly after every position a resequence can assign — the interleaving is correct with no lock, and taking none is what stops this verb inverting the item-then-section nesting a refile already establishes. **§11.4** records both ordering controls on the index; **§16.4** gains the item resequence facts as a paragraph of their own and its counted-class census moves to **twenty-four**. **F-93 needed no edit and that is a finding rather than a relief**: `.record-actions button` has been in the 375px barrier since it was written and matched nothing until these rows rendered the first submit control in an actions cell, so a selector matching nothing was indistinguishable from one matching everything it should. What made it safe is that `.menu-group-actions button` was already asserting the same claim on the same page; the barrier's comment now says so. **No CSS**, on v1.32's reasoning — `.record-actions` has styled a `button` since Slice 30.

No schema change and no migration — this verb writes a type `0001` already declares and `0004` already CHECK-bound. No new read: both directory verbs the surface calls have existed since Slice 40. No ADR edit, no `compose.yaml` edit, no `.slnx` edit, no `REQUIREMENTS.md` edit (rev 6 already asks for this menu), and no `export.sh` edit — **the dump reduction is again specified and deferred by name** in `_CHANGES.md`, unchanged from v1.32's statement of it, because every remaining cut is a split of a history register that four gates read and this slice already touches one of those registers twice. One xUnit analyzer warning is cleared in passing (`xUnit2031`, a `Where` before `Assert.Single` in `MenuSectionResequenceTests`). Predicted test count 1180 → **1190**: eight assertions in `MenuItemResequenceTests`, two in `MenuWiringTests`.

**v1.32 — 2026-08-17.** The runner that was a default, and the verb that could finally be written. Two changes, and the ruling that let them ride together is stated rather than assumed: they fail in *distinguishable* ways. A runner defect stops the suite from building or starting — a `.targets` error, exit code 5, no tests found — and a verb defect is an assertion that fails with a name; §18's habit of chasing a count deviation before a slice closes is only expensive when two candidates produce the *same* symptom, and these cannot.

**The runner (F-97).** `xunit.v3` 4.0.0 installs `xunit.v3.mtp-v2`, MTP 2 removed the VSTest target for the .NET 10 SDK, and this tree was on the VSTest path — so the version bump failed at build time in all four test projects. **§16.4** now states the runner as a contract: Microsoft.Testing.Platform, selected once by the `test` stanza in `global.json`, with `Microsoft.NET.Test.Sdk` and `xunit.runner.visualstudio` **prohibited rather than merely unused** and deleted from all four projects and from the version list. `--logger` is gone from CI and from `scripts/ci_local.sh`; a solution or project is named with `--solution` or `--project`; the TRX report is xUnit.net's own, asked for after the `--` that marks where the test application's arguments begin. `README.md` says why `--project` is not a style preference. The finding is not the build failure, which is loud: it is that the runner was stated in **four independent places** with nothing comparing them, so a tree could be half-migrated and `dotnet test` would mean different things depending on which file was edited last. **§16.4** gains the gate paragraph: `TestRunnerContractTests`, four assertions, subject computed over every project, script and workflow, all four proven sensitive against the pre-fix state — and its package scan reads the `Include` attribute rather than the bare name on **F-67's** standard, because a substring scan reported the very comments that record the deletion. That is the gate catching itself before it shipped.

**The verb (Stage 3a).** **§7** records `ResequenceMenuSectionsAsync`: the **whole ordering** in, `0…n-1` assigned from it, every row locked `FOR UPDATE` **ordered by identifier** so two concurrent resequences cannot deadlock against each other, one `reordered` event per heading that **actually moved**, and a list that is not a permutation of the stored set **refused whole** rather than partially obeyed — a short list, a repeated identifier and an unknown heading are one answer, because from the write's side they are one fact. All of one call's events carry one `occurred_at`, so they read in the order the rows were written *only because* §8.1 requires the identifier factory to ascend inside a millisecond, which is why this verb waited for the slice that made that true (**F-95**) instead of shipping beside it. **§7's recorded cut is closed:** `/administration/menu` moves a heading with Up and Down, each its own static-SSR form named from the heading's identifier, the ends **disabled rather than omitted** because a control that vanishes at the edge of a list moves every other control up a row and §16.3 scenario 16 measures where controls are. The editor keeps the absolute write, and §7 says why that is a different question rather than a duplicate. **F-93's rule is obeyed on the way in for the first time** rather than after the fact: the 375px barrier gains `.menu-group-actions button` in the same slice as the control it measures. **No CSS**, deliberately — `.menu-group-actions .button-secondary` has styled that row since Slice 44 and a `<button>` matches it, so nothing about these controls' height is new. **§16.4** gains the resequence facts as a paragraph of their own and its counted-class census moves to **twenty-three**; two of that section's counts move with it, and the prose census beside one of them is **deleted rather than corrected** (F-77), because "§7's five section verbs" became six in this slice and nothing in the tree could check the sentence.

No schema change and no migration — this verb writes a type `0003` already declares. No ADR edit, no `compose.yaml` edit, no `.slnx` edit, no `REQUIREMENTS.md` edit (rev 6 already asks for this menu, and R§2's test stack is named by framework rather than by adapter package), and no `export.sh` edit: the dump reduction this slice was asked about is **specified and deferred by name** in `_CHANGES.md`, because every remaining cut is a split of a history register that four gates read. Predicted test count 1166 → **1180**: four assertions in `TestRunnerContractTests`, eight in `MenuSectionResequenceTests`, two in `MenuWiringTests`.

**v1.31 — 2026-08-17.** The dump that had become mostly history. No behaviour changes and nothing under `src/` is touched; what changes is the artefact a session reads the tree through. **§18** gains two paragraphs. The build log is now **two files and one log**: `docs/BUILD_PROGRESS.md` holds M6 Slice 40 onward, `docs/progress/BUILD_PROGRESS_THROUGH_M6_SLICE_39.md` holds everything before it byte-exact, and a *BUILD_PROGRESS M6 Slice N* citation resolves by slice number — about a hundred such rows are deliberately **left unrewritten**, because they cite the log rather than a file and the rule is stated once in both headers (F-47). `export.sh` withholds `docs/progress/` from `dump.txt` by path, and the **hazard that creates is normative rather than incidental**: a session cannot see a withheld file, so nothing may reconstruct one and the retained half must never be delivered as the whole document. Two rules follow — **withheld is not exempt** (archived history is authored text, so tree hygiene still checks it and the platform-state rule still exempts it as a record file, which is why the exporter's *generated* and *archived* sets are separate), and **every withheld document is linked by path from a document the dump contains**. The per-file metadata block drops from twelve fields to **three** — relative path, size, SHA-256, the three that make a reconstruction checkable — shedding 164 KiB, four fields of which described the authoring machine; `LICENSE` is elided to metadata and hash, which is safe because the hash still pins the text, and is reverted by deleting one array entry. **§2** records `docs/progress/` and `docs/llm/` in the repository layout. **§16.4** gains one paragraph and its counted-class census moves to **twenty-one**. **Appendix A** gains **F-96**, and it is recorded as a finding rather than an enhancement because the mechanism is general: an artefact regenerated on every run grows without anything deciding it should, and what that costs is invisible until a session runs out of room. Measured on the produced file rather than predicted: **6,377,323 → 5,483,728 bytes, about 14% smaller**, 87% of capacity to about 75%. The saving is stated loosely on purpose — the dump contains the changelog entry stating it, so the artefact measures itself. **The resequencing verb §7 records as an open cut is again deliberately not here**, on the ruling v1.30 made and this version is the second application of — one change, one green run, then the feature: this version moves 730 KiB of documentation that four gates read and changes what a fifth script enumerates, and a red run beside a new write would have two candidate causes. No `REQUIREMENTS.md` edit, no schema change, no migration, no read changed, no surface changed, no ADR edit, no `compose.yaml` edit, no `.slnx` edit, no new package. Predicted test count 1162 → **1166**: four assertions, all in the new `ContextDumpExclusionContractTests`.

**v1.30 — 2026-08-17.** One identifier, nine reads, and a property nobody was keeping. **§8.1** now requires `IIdentifierFactory` to hand out values that **ascend under PostgreSQL's `uuid` ordering even when two land in the same millisecond**, and says why that is a requirement on the factory rather than a property of UUIDv7: `Guid.CreateVersion7()` is `Guid.NewGuid()` with the timestamp overwritten, so it is ordered between milliseconds and random inside one, while every mutation in §8 stamps all the rows of its transaction with one `IClock.UtcNow` and therefore leans on the tie-break constantly. **F-95** is the finding and it is the one to read: §11.4's per-item history read a three-event create in the minted order one time in six, the activity feed §11.4 gained last slice reordered itself between page loads on unchanged data, and **a guest's basket order did not survive being sent**, because `OrderProjection` breaks its line tie on `order_line_identifier` and `OrderStaging.Build` mints those in a tight loop in basket order. The suite reported it once, as a single inverted pair, and passed on the rerun — which is what a 50% property looks like from inside a green run. **§16.4** gains one paragraph, and its counted-class census moves to twenty. **No schema change, no migration, no read changed, no surface changed**, and no `REQUIREMENTS.md` edit: this is a mechanism that was already required to work catching up with itself. **ADR-0011** records the counter and its two edges. `MenuEventLog`'s two ordering comments are repointed at the contract instead of at the format; `AdministrationMenu.razor` needed no edit, because what its `@key` comment claims is now true. **The reordering verb §7 records as an open cut is deliberately not here** — it writes several `reordered` events in one transaction at one instant, which is precisely this property, so shipping it in the same slice would make a red run unable to say which change caused it. Predicted test count 1157 → **1162**: five assertions, all in `UuidV7IdentifierFactoryTests`.

**v1.29 — 2026-08-17.** The index becomes the menu. **§7** records `/administration/menu` as **sections-first**: a group per heading, a `<details>` rendered open, its summary carrying the name, the visibility chip, the item count and the position, its body carrying that heading's items as §11.12's record list. The flat item list with a heading *column* is gone — it was shipped deliberately as an honest intermediate and named as one, because a record list whose rows link nowhere is a list of dead ends. Two rulings are recorded rather than left to be inferred. **It is rendered open on every request**, because a heading a server collapsed is a heading whose items nobody looking for an item can find, and because §16.3 scenario 16 measures what a layout engine laid out — a control inside a closed `<details>` has no box, so a collapsed group would silently withdraw its own controls from the barrier that exists to catch exactly that. **An empty heading is visible here and nowhere else in the application**: the old list was built from `menu_item`, so a heading with nothing under it was on no page at all, and the page now reads both directories — headings from `IMenuSectionDirectory`, items from `IMenuDirectory`, filtered per heading rather than grouped, because the six-key ordering already makes each heading's items contiguous. **Reordering a heading is deliberately not on this surface**, and §7 says why rather than leaving it to be discovered: `ReorderMenuSectionAsync` sets an *absolute* `display_order` and positions are non-unique with a name tie-break, so “move this heading up” is not expressible as one absolute write, and an honest up/down control needs a resequencing verb writing several rows and several `reordered` events in one transaction — a new write with new event semantics rather than a surface change. **§11.4** describes the index. **§16.3's scenario 17** gains a final step whose assertion is a **disagreement**: a third heading is created and left empty, and the same instant must produce three groups on the administrator's index and two groupings on the guest's menu, with the difference being exactly the empty one — reading either surface alone says nothing, because a heading missing from the guest's menu has three possible reasons and a heading present on the index has none. **§16.4** gains two paragraphs. **Appendix A** gains **F-93** and **F-94**. **F-93 is the one to read**: the 375px barrier decides what it measures from a list of class names, so a surface can keep being *visited* and stop being *measured* with nothing reporting it — this index replaces `.record-actions` rows the barrier read with `.menu-group` groups it did not, and the floor above it is structurally blind, because a floor notices a selector group that vanished and never one that was never counted. It was caught in the slice that would have caused it, and the repair is a **rule** in §16.4 rather than two selectors in a harness: a surface that acquires a new kind of control acquires a selector in the same slice, or it is a surface the barrier has stopped asserting anything about. **F-94** is the old index's closing sentence, which said *across N sections* and counted headings that had something under them — with a comment one line above it accurately describing the discrepancy instead of fixing it, which is F-65's mechanism in a component. **No new assertion and no new test class**, so §16.4's counted-class census and its floor of nineteen are untouched, and the predicted test count does not move: scenario 17 is **extended, not added**, so §16.3 stays at seventeen. No `REQUIREMENTS.md` edit, on the v1.2 reasoning: rev 6 already asks for this menu and for a program operable on the screen §1 says it is used from. **No schema change** — `0005` is unchanged, no migration is added, and no read is added either: both directory verbs this page calls have existed since Slice 40. No ADR edit, no `compose.yaml` edit, no `.slnx` edit, no new package.

**v1.28 — 2026-08-16.** The last verb, and three numbers written where nothing could check them. **§7** records `MoveMenuItemToSectionAsync`: an item can be refiled under another heading, it is **appended to the end** of that heading at `MAX + 1` under a lock on the target section row, and because §8.2 binds `new_display_order` to `reordered` alone a move that also changes the position writes a **second** event — conditional on the number actually differing, on the no-op rule the other verbs already follow. Nothing else about the item moves, so an 86'd dish refiled between headings is still 86'd, which is the item-side counterpart of a deactivated heading not cascading to its items. It publishes as loudly as a section visibility flip, because §11.1 groups the guest menu by heading and a refile *into an inactive heading* removes the card from the guest's menu entirely. **With it, no verb behind `IMenuWorkflow` is without a surface** — the obligation that governed six verbs across seven slices is discharged rather than narrowed, and it could be stated as discharged because the outstanding verb was named every slice instead of quietly omitted. **§11.4** names the picker on an item's own page and records that inactive headings are offered there and marked. **§16.3's scenario 17** gains a final step: an item is refiled through that picker and a guest who touched nothing watches the card change groupings and land **behind** the dish already under the destination heading — the only place §7's append-on-move rule is observed through a browser. **§16.4** gains a paragraph and corrects two counts. **Appendix A** gains **F-90**, **F-91** and **F-92**, which are one defect three times and the reason they ship together. **F-90 is the one to read**: the predicted test count was 1149 and the run returned 1151, and the two account for each other exactly — §16.4 said *four theory rows* where `0005` had made it six. `TestingSectionContractTests` is not at fault and no gate is added for it; that gate compares an **assertion count per class**, which for that file is 7 and is still right, and a theory-row count is a different quantity in the same paragraph. So a number went stale inside the one section written to stop numbers going stale, in the one form its gate is structurally blind to. **F-91** is the handheld barrier's expected-control census, itemised in a comment, wrong since Slice 38, and unreportable because the assertion above it is a floor — a floor that passes at fifteen also passes at seventeen. The floor is deliberately **not raised**: its value is a claim about controls rendered on ten surfaces, which the authoring environment cannot observe, and an unobservable raise is the edit that turns a green suite red for no gain. **F-92** is this document's own opening sentence, citing `REQUIREMENTS.md` rev 5 since v1.15 moved it to rev 6. All three are repaired the same way — **the number is deleted, not corrected** (F-77) — and in all three the residual is stated rather than closed, because a gate for one sentence leaves every other instance of the class untouched (F-47). **§18's habit found F-90**, on the first run in three slices where it was possible to run. **No `REQUIREMENTS.md` edit**, on the v1.2 reasoning: rev 6 already asks for this menu and R§6.8 has required an append-only mirror of every menu change since rev 1. **No schema change** — `0005` is unchanged and no migration is added; the move writes types `0005` already declared. No ADR edit, no `compose.yaml` edit, no `.slnx` edit, no new package, and no CSS: `.manage-inline-form` has styled `select` since Slice 34, which is why the picker needed no rule of its own.

**v1.27 — 2026-08-16.** Seven defects, one shape, and a habit that did not hold. Slice 41 shipped an archive that did not compile and whose integration suite could not have run if it had; this version records what was in it and changes no behaviour anybody uses. **§16.4** gains three paragraphs and corrects one. The corrected one is `MenuAdministrationTests`: a description supplied at creation writes a *further* event rather than a *second* one, because `0005` made the heading mandatory and always logged, and that paragraph had been describing a create one row smaller than the one this tree performs. **Appendix A** gains **F-83** through **F-89**. Six of the seven are one mechanism seen from six angles — **a schema widened by a migration reaches the test arrangement last**, because arrangement is the code nobody is looking at while implementing the thing being arranged for. `OrderTestWorld`'s event INSERT named two payload columns where §8.2 has five, so five of the eight admitted types could be written and three could not (**F-86**); six assertions in `MenuAdministrationTests` were counting a create that had grown a row, and two of those were reading a payload off the newest event, which after `0005` is the section event and is null in every payload column by CHECK (**F-87**); `MenuItemSummary` grew from seven members to ten and a positional stand-in in the staging tests did not (**F-84**); a two-character username met `person.username`'s three-character CHECK and took all six facts of a class down in `InitializeAsync` (**F-85**); and an end-to-end harness read a heading with `InnerText` where `app.css` declares `text-transform: uppercase`, so it compared a rendering against a name (**F-88**). **F-83 is the one worth reading.** The gate that repairs F-80 named `EventTypeVocabulary`; the type has always been `EventTypeCatalogue`, and the wrong name came from F-80's own rows in this document and in `DOCUMENTATION_REVIEW.md`, so four documents agreed with each other and none with the tree. The gate therefore did not compile, and for one slice the vocabulary was simultaneously correct in `EventExplorerReads.cs` and guarded by nothing — the third consecutive slice whose real cost was the gates downstream of a build failure reporting nothing. **No gate is added for any of the seven**, which is F-71's ruling on its second application and F-47's throughout: the compiler refused two of them and PostgreSQL's CHECK constraints refused two more, loudly, at the first opportunity, and a test re-asserting what CSC or a CHECK already rejects is a monument. **F-89 reverses a ruling**, which is rare enough to say plainly. F-73 kept a census count in prose on the ground that it was the argument for `MinimumCountedClasses`, adding the habit of moving it; across the two census moves that followed, both prose copies were left behind — this section said ten while the floor said eighteen, and the gate's own summary said sixteen in a sentence whose next clause said eighteen. Three slices of evidence beat one ruling, so F-77's habit wins: both prose copies are deleted and the floor is the only copy, because it is enforced on every run and can therefore only go stale loudly. The floor moves from eighteen to nineteen with the census, `MenuEventVocabularyContractTests` having become countable by acquiring a §16.4 paragraph of its own. **No `REQUIREMENTS.md` edit**, on the v1.2 reasoning. **No schema change** — `0005` is unchanged and no migration is added. No ADR edit, no `compose.yaml` edit, no `.slnx` edit, no new package, and nothing under `src/` changes behaviour: the two edits there are comments that asserted the opposite of the `switch` arms twenty lines beneath them.

**v1.26 — 2026-08-16.** The heading that could finally be edited, a reserved word two files were named after, and a gate that never ran. **§7** records the section editor and closes an obligation carried since `0003`: all five of `IMenuSectionAdministration`'s verbs now have a surface and all five are behind `IMenuWorkflow`, the four new ones arriving together because they are four forms on one page and a subset would have left the same hole under a smaller name. Two of them publish for reasons the item verbs do not have — §11.1 renders a heading above every card under it, so a rename that announced nothing leaves the old word on every open picker; and §7 hides an inactive heading from the guest **entirely**, so a switch-off without a broadcast leaves a whole part of the menu tappable on every phone until the send is refused for a reason the guest never saw coming. A section **description** publishes too and reaches no guest surface today, on the same ruling the item description already carries. §7 also records `IMenuSectionEventLog` as a **second reader** rather than a widening of the item log — the two vocabularies share three words and mean different things by all three — with no cross-section activity feed, because a read with no caller is the same defect as a workflow verb with no caller; and that a section's `created` carries **all three** payloads where an item's carries two, which is the fact most easily got backwards. **§11.4** names the editor and its per-section stream. **§16.3's scenario 17** regains the two steps Slice 40 drafted and cut: a heading switched off through the editor leaves an already-open guest menu with everything under it, the other heading stays present and orderable, and switching it back on restores the menu exactly as it was — which is also the only end-to-end proof that deactivating a heading **does not cascade** to its items. **§16.4** gains four paragraphs and corrects four counts. **Appendix A** gains **F-81** — two loop variables named `section`, which is MVC's section directive, producing four build errors that name the directive and never the identifier, invisible in review because `@key="section.…"` one line away compiles perfectly, and blocking everywhere because every project references the one that failed. It is made executable rather than remembered: `RazorDirectiveContractTests` refuses both unusable directives across the component tree, and proves itself sensitive against synthesised markup rather than against the tree, since the first fact must survive the repair. **Appendix A** also gains **F-82**: Slice 40 moved four assertion counts in the files and none in this document, and `TestingSectionContractTests` — written after F-70 for exactly that drift — reported nothing, because F-81 stopped the project it lives in from compiling. **F-71's lesson from the other side**: a gate that cannot run is indistinguishable from a gate that passed, so the first question about a red build is what stopped being checked rather than only what stopped compiling. The floor moves from sixteen to eighteen with the census (F-73's habit, third application). No `REQUIREMENTS.md` edit, on the v1.2 reasoning: rev 6 already asks for this menu and R§6.8 has required an append-only mirror of every menu change since rev 1. No schema change — `0005` is unchanged and no migration is added. No `compose.yaml` edit, no `.slnx` edit, no new package.

**v1.25 — 2026-08-16.** The heading every item now has, and a vocabulary that had been wrong for two migrations. **§7** records `0005` and closes the enhancement request's schema half: `menu_item.menu_section_identifier uuid NOT NULL REFERENCES menu_section`, `section_changed` with its payload column and a fifth named biconditional, a create-section page, a required picker on the create-item form, and §11.1's guest menu **grouped under its headings** — which is the part the person who made the request could see and the part two migrations existed to make possible.
